Now the Kogge-Stone adder module — write it cleanly:

```systemverilog
module ks_adder #(
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH = 21
) (
    input  logic [WIDTH-1:0] a_i,
    input  logic [WIDTH-1:0] b_i,
    output logic [WIDTH-1:0] s_o
);
    localparam int unsigned LEVELS = $clog2(WIDTH);   // 5 for 21
    logic [WIDTH-1:0] g [0:LEVELS];
    logic [WIDTH-1:0] p [0:LEVELS];

    assign g[0] = a_i & b_i;
    assign p[0] = a_i ^ b_i;

    for (genvar l = 0; l < LEVELS; l++) begin : g_ks
        for (genvar i = 0; i < WIDTH; i++) begin : g_bits
            if (i >= (1 << l)) begin : g_combine
                assign g[l+1][i] = g[l][i] | (p[l][i] & g[l][i - (1 << l)]);
                assign p[l+1][i] = p[l][i] | p[l][i - (1 << l)];  // Kogge-Stone: p stays p (OR is fine but unnecessary)
            end else begin : g_pass
                assign g[l+1][i] = g[l][i];
                assign p[l+1][i] = p[l][i];
            end
        end
    end

    assign s_o = g[LEVELS] ^ p[LEVELS] ... 
```

Hmm wait, Kogge-Stone: after LEVELS=log2(WIDTH) levels, g[l][i] = generate of block [i-2^l+1, i] (or 0 if i < 2^l-1... for i < 2^l, g stays just the single-bit generate). Final carry into bit i = g[LEVELS][i-1] (prefix generate up to i-1). s_o[i] = p[0][i] ^ (i==0 ? 1'b0 : g[LEVELS][i-1]).

But with WIDTH=21 not a power of 2: LEVELS = 5 (2^5=32 ≥ 21). At level l, i - 2^l could be negative for i ≥ 2^l only... i >= (1<<l) ensures i - 2^l >= 0 ✓. But Kogge-Stone correctness for non-power-of-2: after 5 levels, g[5][i] = gen of [i-31, i] clipped to [0,i] — that IS the full prefix ✓ (standard: Kogge-Stone with WIDTH bits, log2(WIDTH) rounded up levels gives complete prefixes for all i). Yes: g_l[i] = generate of bits [max(0, i-2^l+1), i]. After l = ceil(log2(W)) = 5: [max(0,i-31), i] = [0,i] ✓.

p doesn't need updating across levels for the final sum (only g propagates via p[l][i] = a[i]^b[i] original — in Kogge-Stone, the p used at each level must be the LEVEL's p (p[l][i] = propagate of the same block as g[l][i])). Standard KS: g[l+1][i] = g[l][i] | (p[l][i] & g[l][i-2^l]); p[l+1][i] = p[l][i] & p[l][i-2^l]. If I skip updating p (keep p[l+1] = p[l]), then p[l][i] = p[0][i] = single-bit propagate — WRONG for l ≥ 1? g[l][i] = gen of block ending at i; the term p must be the propagate of that SAME block [i-2^l+1, i]. p[0][i] is only bit i. Hmm — for l=1: g[2][i] = g[1][i] | (p[1][i] & g[1][i-2]). p[1][i] must be propagate of [i-1,i] = p0[i]&p0[i-1]. So I DO need to update p: p[l+1][i] = p[l][i] & p[l][i-2^l]. Let me include that. (Using & for propagate is standard; OR also works for g-computation but & is needed... no wait: for the generate recurrence p is used as "propagate through the block", must be AND. Yes use &.)

Level 5 for WIDTH=21: i-2^5 = i-32 < 0 for all i<32 → all pass-through; so LEVELS=5 gives g[5] = g[4] = blocks of 16. WRONG for i ≥ 16!     // ---- final combine: csa_sum + csa_carry + popcount constant ----
    // One 3:2 compressor level folds the popcount into the carry-save form,
    // then a Kogge-Stone prefix adder does the single carry-propagate add.
    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] fc_a, fc_b, fc_g;
    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] fc_s2;

The two's complement: operand_value = sign ? (~operand+1) : operand — 16+1 = 17 incrementers of 7 bits (serial chain!). TRICK: skip the increment — the increment's purpose: operand_value = |operand| (two's complement magnitude). But the downstream LZC and regime extraction... hmm, LZC(~x + 1) vs LZC(~x): for the REGIME field we count leading 1s-or-0s of the two's complement value. ~x+1 vs ~x differ only in trailing bits (carry propagates from LSB through trailing 1s of ~x = trailing 0s of x). The LEADING bits are identical unless x is all-1s (i.e., operand = -1 → ~x+1 = 0 → vs ~x = 0xFFFE...). So the LZC result differs only in the all-ones edge case... but the MANTISSA needs the exact two's complement (op_no_rg = operand_value << regime_bits). The mantissa norm: {implicit_bit, op_no_rg[n-2-es:2]} = bits of the shifted value — the increment DOES affect those bits (through the trailing-1 carry... only within the trailing run). Hmm, the carry from +1 propagates through the trailing 1s of ~x; those are LOW bits; the mantissa takes bits [n-2-es:2] of op_no_rg = high-ish bits — a carry into bit 2+ changes them only if the trailing-1 run extends past bit 1. Possible (x = ...111000 pattern → ~x = ...000111 → +1 = ...001000: carry propagates through bits 0-2). So the mantissa DOES differ. Can't skip the increment safely. BUT — the +1 can be FUSED into the barrel shift: shift-then-increment? (x<<s)+1 vs ((x+1)<<s)? No... operand_value = ~x + 1; then op_no_rg = operand_value << regime_bits = (~x + 1) << s = (~x)<<s + (1<<s). The barrel shifter shifts ~x by s, then we add 1<<s — an increment at position s — position depends on s — messy but: (1<<s) addition = setting bit s + carry propagation... equivalent to adding a 1-hot. Alternatively fuse into the NEXT adder downstream (the exp/mantissa handling)? The mantissa is just extracted (no add). Hmm, the carry from +(1<<s) propagates upward through the bits of (~x)<<s — bits above s. Not fusible cheaply.

```
assign sign_ab = {operand_a[WIDTH],operand_b[WIDTH]};
case(sign_ab)
    2'b10: result_o = operand_b;
    2'b01: result_o = operand_a;
    default: result_o = (data_a>data_b) ? operand_a : operand_b;
```
data_a = operand_a[WIDTH-1:0] (unsigned compare!). For both negative (11): picks the one with the larger lower-bit pattern. For two's complement negatives: -1 = 1...1111 (largest pattern) → -1 is the max, and its pattern IS the largest ✓! For negatives, larger magnitude = more negative = ... the pattern of -x is 2^W − x → larger pattern = SMALLER x = less negative = LARGER signed value ✓✓. So unsigned pattern compare on the lower bits + same sign = correct signed compare ✓. And mixed signs ✓. So the comparator IS a correct signed max ✓. Therefore plain `signed >` works identically (value-wise) ✓.

Current stage E code:
```
    logic final_sign;
    logic [SUM_WIDTH-1:0] sum_c;
    
    assign final_sign = pipe4_sum_result[SUM_WIDTH];
    assign sum_c = final_sign ? (~pipe4_sum_result+1) : pipe4_sum_result[SUM_WIDTH-1:0];

    // ---------------
    // Mantissa normalization and exponent adjustment
    // ---------------
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] rg_exp_adjust;
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] final_rg_exp;
    logic [SUM_WIDTH-1:0] sum_norm;

    mantissa_norm #(
        .WIDTH(SUM_WIDTH),
        .EXP_WIDTH(EXP_WIDTH),
        .DECIMAL_POINT(CARRY_WIDTH+2)
    ) u_mantissa_norm(
        .operand_i(sum_c),
        .exp_adjust(rg_exp_adjust),
        .result_o(sum_norm)
    );
    assign final_rg_exp = pipe4_rg_exp_max + rg_exp_adjust;

    logic [MANT_WIDTH_O+2:0] final_mant;
    
    if(SUM_WIDTH>MANT_WIDTH_O+3) begin
        logic [SUM_WIDTH-MANT_WIDTH_O-3:0] sticky_bits;
        logic sticky_bit;
        assign sticky_bits = sum_norm[SUM_WIDTH-MANT_WIDTH_O-3:0];
        assign sticky_bit = |sticky_bits;
        assign final_mant = {sum_norm[SUM_WIDTH-1:SUM_WIDTH-MANT_WIDTH_O-2],sticky_bit};
    end
    else begin
        assign final_mant = sum_norm << (MANT_WIDTH_O+3-SUM_WIDTH);
    end
```
SUM_WIDTH=19, MANT_WIDTH_O=11: SUM_WIDTH−MANT_WIDTH_O−3 = 5 → sticky_bits = sum_norm[5:0] (6 bits); final_mant = {sum_norm[18:6], sticky} (13+1 = 14 bits ✓ [MANT_WIDTH_O+2:0] = [13:0] ✓).

Wait — the lzc module's cnt width: CNT_WIDTH = idx_width(19) = clog(19) = 5 ✓. lead_zero is 5 bits [4:0].

exp_adjust = sum_zeroes ? 0 : (DECIMAL_POINT−1−L)?? From the module: if lzc_zeroes → 0; elif L ≤ DECIMAL_POINT−1 (6) → DECIMAL_POINT−L−1 = 6−L; else −(L−DECIMAL_POINT+1) = −(L−6) = 6−L. So exp_adjust = sum_zeroes ? 0 : 6−L (signed, EXP_WIDTH+1 = 7 bits). ✓ (verify: L=7 → −1 = 6−7 ✓.)

Fused final_mant:
```
    logic [4:0] lead_zero;  // LZC output

    endgenerate
```
Hmm — `lead_zero == g[4:0]` — fine.

final_mant[k] for k=1..13: sum_c index = 19−k−ℓ where ℓ = L. Let me define off = 19−k (13 values: 18..6). final_mant[k] = OR_ℓ oh[ℓ] & (off−ℓ ≥ 0 ? sum_c[off−ℓ] : 0). For k=1..13, off=19−k; ℓ ranges 0..18; off−ℓ ∈ [−?..off]. For off−ℓ < 0 → 0. Note also: when ℓ > off, sum_norm bit = 0 ✓.

So: final_mant[13:1][j] where j = k−1 ∈ [0,12]: bit j of the field = OR_ℓ oh[ℓ] & sum_c[(18−j)−ℓ]. Equivalently: define rev: field[j] = Σ_ℓ oh[ℓ]·sum_c[18−j−ℓ] — a correlation = for each j, the 19-bit window of sum_c starting at 18−j going down... note field[j] for j=12 uses sum_c[6−ℓ], j=0 uses sum_c[18−ℓ]. Hmm interesting: field[j] = sum_norm[18−j] ✓ consistent.

sticky = OR_ℓ (oh[ℓ] & sticky_ℓ) where sticky_ℓ = (ℓ ≤ 5) ? |sum_c[5−ℓ:0] : 0. Precompute prefix-ORs: sor[i] = |sum_c[i:0]; sticky = OR_ℓ (oh[ℓ] & sor[5−ℓ]) for ℓ=0..5.

Then final_mant = {field[12:0], sticky}?? wait ordering: final_mant[13:1] = sum_norm[18:6] → final_mant[13] = sum_norm[18] = field[0] (j=0 ↔ bit 18), ..., final_mant[1] = sum_norm[6] = field[12] ✓. So final_mant = {field[0], field[1], ..., field[12], sticky} ✓ (field[j] at position 13−j).

Implementation sketch:
```
    logic [SUM_WIDTH-1:0] oh;
    logic [SUM_WIDTH+MANT_WIDTH_O+1:0] ...
    generate

    endgenerate
```
Hmm — declaring per-iteration wires inside generate blocks: `if (1) begin : blk logic [..] t; ... end` — yosys handles named blocks with locals ✓ (the existing code does this in comp_tree). Alternatively write it with functions:

```
    function automatic logic fm_bit(input integer j);
        logic [SUM_WIDTH-1:0] acc;
        acc = '0;
        for (integer l2 = 0; l2 < SUM_WIDTH; l2++)
            if (18 - j - l2 >= 0)
                acc = acc | ({SUM_WIDTH{oh[l2]}} & ({{(18-j-l2+1){1'b0}}, ... }));
```
Getting messy with variable part-selects. Alternative formulation without one-hot: field[j] = sum_norm[18−j] where sum_norm = sum_c << L. Rewrite directly: field[j] = (18−j ≥ L) ? sum_c[18−j−L] : 0 — a CONDITIONAL part-select with variable index: sum_c[18−j−L] — Verilog allows variable bit-select: sum_c[idx] where idx is an expression ✓ (but idx must be in range or returns x — guard with the conditional). A variable bit-select synthesizes to a 19:1 mux per bit — SAME as the barrel shifter (that's exactly what it is!). The one-hot AND-OR version is the fast form. The variable-index mux would map to the same 5-level mux chain — no gain. So I need the explicit AND-OR.

Let me structure it with generate + localparam-free logic using a 2-D wire array:

```
    wire [SUM_WIDTH-1:0] sc_rev;   // sum_c reversed? 
```
Hmm — alternative trick: field[j] = OR_ℓ oh[ℓ] & sum_c[18−j−ℓ]. Define for each ℓ: shifted_ℓ[j] = sum_c[18−j−ℓ] = (sum_c >> (ℓ − (18−...))) hmm: as j increases by 1, the index decreases by 1 → shifted_ℓ = sum_c reversed then shifted?? Let rev[i] = sum_c[18−i] (bit-reverse, wiring). Then shifted_ℓ[j] = rev[18−(18−j−ℓ)] = rev[j+ℓ]... check: rev[j+ℓ] = sum_c[18−j−ℓ] ✓!! So field[j] = OR_ℓ oh[ℓ] & rev[j+ℓ] — a triangular AND-OR: for each j (0..12), OR over ℓ (0..18−j−... index j+ℓ ≤ 18): field[j] = OR_{ℓ=0..18−j} oh[ℓ] & rev[j+ℓ].

So: rev = bit-reversed sum_c (pure wiring). field[j] = Σ_ℓ oh[ℓ]·rev[j+ℓ] — this is a matrix-vector product with an anti-triangular 0/1 matrix: 13 rows × (19−j) terms. Total AND terms = Σ (19−j) = 13×19 − 78 = 169 ANDs + 13 OR-trees (up to 19 inputs ≈ 3 levels). Delay: oh decode (1 level) + AND (1) + OR-tree (~3) ≈ 4-5 levels ≈ 150-180ps vs barrel 258 ✓ save ~80-100ps. Area: 169 AND + ~150 OR ≈ 320 cells vs barrel ~190 → +130 cells ≈ +?? at 286 area, maybe +10-15 area units. ADP: 296×640 ≈ 189k vs 215k → −12%. Hmm OK. ## Summary

**Final result: PASS, best cost 9.68e5 area_delay_product** — which corresponds to the **original `starting_point.v`** (area 1063.5, delay 910.2). Important: the working file `starting_point.v` was left **broken** by my last edit cycle (132,022/2M vector failures) — see §4. `newdesign.v` is a stray placeholder; ignore it.

### 1. Approaches tried
- **Baseline eval** of the original 5-stage pipelined PDPU: PASS, 968k (area 1063 × delay 910).
- **Multiplier swap**: replaced 16 radix-4 Booth multipliers (4×4 mantissa multiply) with plain `*`. Exact same results, less logic.
- **Negate folding**: replaced the 17 per-row two's-complement negate incrementers with a one's-complement XOR (invert) plus a popcount-of-sign-mask constant added in the final carry-propagate adder. Mathematically exact mod 2^20.
- **Stage rebalancing**: moved exp-diff subtract/saturate into stage B (registered shift amounts into pipe2), products into pipe2, keeping the 14-bit barrel shifters alone in stage C.
- **3-way comparator max tree**: replaced the recursive pairwise `comp_tree` (17 inputs, 5 levels) with a 3-level max3 tree using plain `$signed >` (value-equivalent; tie-breaks don't matter since only the max *value* is used).
- **Kogge-Stone prefix adder** for the final 3-input combine (3:2 compress then one prefix add).
- **Wallace popcount** (FA/HA column compression) for the sign-mask popcount.
- **target_delay sweep**: at default 500 the tool chased an unreachable constraint using huge cells. Relaxing to 4000–5000 shrank area ~3× at flat ~750ps structural delay (e.g. 4500 → area ~287, cost ~215k; 5000 → 262k; 10000 → 426k). This was the single biggest lever in late-stage gains.

### 2. Most impactful
- The `target_delay` relaxation (default 500 was far below the ~730ps structural floor) — several-fold cost reduction with zero RTL change.
- Structural: negate folding + 3-way max tree + Kogge-Stone adder together cut the worst stage from ~955ps to ~730ps and area from 1063 to ~922 (cost 968k → ~678k before the target sweep).

### 3. What didn't work / regressed
- **Moving exp-diff/saturate after the compare tree without rebalancing**: overloaded one stage (1138ps) — worse than baseline.
- **18-row CSA tree** (popcount appended as an 18th operand): the recursive `csa_tree` truncates carry bits at each 4:2 level; adding a row changed the truncation pattern → 6.6% mismatch. Must keep 17 rows and add the popcount in the final adder.
- **Sequential popcount loop** (`neg_count = neg_count + 1` in always_comb) built a ripple chain → stage D became critical.
- **Fused one-hot AND-OR field select** replacing the LZC+barrel shifter in stage E: functionally wrong (132k failures) — my index math had a subtle error, and my subsequent "revert" was incomplete.

### 4. Lessons learned
- **Verify every intermediate edit with an eval** — I made several edits in one turn and mis-tracked which registers existed, spending many steps chasing a phantom bug.
- **Keep a saved known-good copy before risky edits** (I had none; the final broken state is unrecoverable without re-doing edits). The known-good optimized configuration was: plain `*` multiply, 17-row tree + popcount in final adder, 3-way max tree, Kogge-Stone final add, Wallace popcount, eval at target_delay ≈ 4500.
- Replicate the original's **CSA-tree truncation semantics exactly** — the oracle shares them, so any tree restructuring is a bit-exactness gamble.
- Cost = area × achieved delay: if the constraint is unmeetable, relaxing `target_delay` is nearly free area.
